### [139] ASSISTANT · 2026-08-12 13:37:25 UTC

```
Done. All deliverables written and validated.

## What I found

**The novelty question, first — it's the highest-stakes item.** arXiv's abstract-field search returns **zero** results for `"critical slowing down" AND "language model"`. No prior work applies EWS to LLM generative dynamics. But two things narrow the claim and must be cited: **arXiv:2605.09043** applies critical-slowing-down signatures to *conversation derailment in human dialogue* (ACL 2026 SRW), and **AQI (arXiv:2506.13901)** is a fifth competitor in our exact niche that the hypothesis doesn't cite.

**Two decisions that change experiment scope:**
- **RAS overlap with our panel is EMPTY.** Every RAS-scored checkpoint is ≥4B; none is ours. We must write "our RAS reimplementation" throughout.
- **AMS overlap is NOT empty** — Llama-3.2-3B/1B-Instruct and gemma-2-2b-it are all in AMS Table I with published σ, giving a real validation gate.

**The discriminating cut is pinned.** Qi's Figure-1 KL values aren't in the text, so I didn't invent them. What *is* pinned is their own operationalisation — `β_t = 2` for `t ≤ 5`, `0.1` for `t > 5`. That gives a defensible k=5, hence **PR-1: Δλ must survive beyond generated step 15**.

**Where I diverged from the plan, and why.** I couldn't confirm the `−(1+3ρ)/n` attribution in a primary source, so rather than paraphrase it from memory I measured it (`estimator_check.py`, fixed seed). That turned out to be the more valuable move: it quantified the confound the plan anticipated (**a 192→64 length difference alone manufactures a ~0.04 spurious AC1 gap in the "right" direction**) and surfaced two hazards the plan didn't — the AR(1)→λ conversion is convex, so λ is **inflated 75%** at n=64/φ=0.9, exactly the slow-recovery regime CSD predicts; and noise-floor truncation **under-estimates λ by 40%** if the fit window overruns.

**Audit: all 16 anchors resolve, none fabricated.** Both load-bearing claims survive verbatim — Kwon's base-model control (64%→25%) and Ratnakar's "~40% depth" — so H1 and Step 0(a) need no rewriting. The unanchored knowledge-action-gap result is **found: arXiv:2603.18353**, both numbers verbatim.

Two corrections for downstream: Qi's "ICLR 2025 **Oral**" is not verifiable from arXiv, and RAS's speed-up is internally inconsistent in the source (216.88× in text vs 210.13× in Table 2 — mean-of-ratios vs ratio-of-means).

**One recommendation beyond the plan:** promote **SRI (arXiv:2602.02600)** to a baseline. It's a per-generated-step scalar built from hidden states we're already extracting — structurally our nearest rival, and nearly free.

Nine items are marked `UNVERIFIED` in the report rather than paraphrased, and §7 lists them explicitly so no gap is silent. The `raw/` directory (1.7 MB of cached fetches) is excluded from the repo upload as scratch.
```

```
Both schema errors and all three content warnings are fixed.

**Schema errors:**
- Added `title` ("Spec Sheets for Rival LLM Safety Metrics", 40 chars — above the minimum that triggered the "too short" warning).
- Added `summary` (1,880 chars) covering the four baseline specs, the four settled decisions, the measured estimator corrections, and the audit outcome.

**Uncited sources {13, 17, 22}** — these were real gaps in the answer prose, not just bookkeeping, so I fixed them by adding the substance rather than tacking on citation markers:
- **[22]** Scheffer et al. 2009 (Nature) now anchors the EWS lineage sentence, which previously jumped straight to the Dakos methods paper without naming its origin.
- **[17]** Krone, Wichers & Hamaker now grounds the *direction* of the AC1 bias — this genuinely strengthened the argument, because it separates "the bias direction is documented in the literature" from "the exact analytic form is unconfirmed, so I measured it."
- **[13]** Xiong et al. now appears in the audit with the >80% ASR figure and the "safety margin" framing, which is what licenses H2b's double-sided reading.

**One thing worth flagging:** the verifier initially reported dangling citations `[0]` and `[48]`. Those were false positives from its citation parser reading mathematical interval notation — `t ∈ [16, 48]` (the PR-1 evaluation window) and `[0,1]` (SRI's range) — as citation markers. I rewrote both as prose ("generated steps 16 through 48", "bounded between 0 and 1") so the parse is unambiguous. Worth knowing for downstream steps: interval notation in the answer field will trip this check.

Final state — all 22 sources cited, no dangling references, `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json` re-synced from the corrected file. `research_report.md` (the primary dossier) was unaffected and retains its interval notation, which is correct there since it isn't parsed for citations.
```
